3SHA · research bench

What holds up when you actually check.

One idea, 648 ways to trade it, put through the same honest examination on every market. Most of them fail — and knowing exactly which ones, and where, is worth more than any single good result.

4markets tested
2,592backtests run
289settings that held
1 / 8signals built

The terrain

every variation, at a glance

Each mark is one complete backtest. Green survived across separate stretches of history; amber held only partly; pale ones didn't make it. A real edge shows up as a band of green, not a lone bright dot.

What gets tested

the fixed plan

Every market is put through this identical grid, so results can be compared instead of each one being quietly tuned to look good.

SHA smoothing (base + HTF1)
2,4 · 3,6 · 4,8 · 6,11 · 8,16 · 10,20
How much the trend layers are smoothed. Smaller reacts sooner but whipsaws more; larger is calmer but later.
Exit trigger (alignment break)
base flip · HTF1 flip · HTF2 flip
Which timeframe turning against the trade closes it. Base is tightest, HTF2 loosest.
Initial stop anchor
Swing · HTF1 Body · HTF2 Body
What the stop is placed behind -- recent structure, or the mid/high timeframe trend body.
Anchor basis
Body · Wick
Body uses the candle body edge (tighter). Wick uses the full high/low including the shadow (wider).
Stop style
mental · hard
Mental only triggers when a candle CLOSES beyond the stop. Hard is a real order that fires intra-bar and survives gaps.
Risk per trade
0.5% · 1.0% · 2.0%
Percent of the account risked from entry to stop. Scales return and drawdown together.
EntryFull Alignment, Confirmed (1 bar) -- longs and shorts
HTF2 smoothing2,2 (held fixed)
Break-evenon -- stop moves to entry at +1R
Trailing stopon -- swing/6 bars, same basis, starts at +1R, close-based
Partial take-profitoff
Gap skipoff
Equity cap100% (so risk% is the only sizing lever)
Stop buffer0.05% of median price (scale-relative, so it means the same on any instrument)
Cost0.1% per round trip
Validationfull history + 5 equal time blocks; ranked on block consistency, not raw return

The signals

1 of 8 built

Each entry type is built and proven on its own before any of them are combined.

Full alignment

All three timeframes agree for the first time.

live

Partial alignment

Two of the three agree.

planned

Full cluster cross

All three bodies cross together.

planned

Pullback resume

Re-entry after a dip inside a live trend.

planned

Early trend

The first turn, before full agreement.

planned

Trend continuation

Adds while a trend is already running.

planned

High-timeframe cross

Price crosses the slow trend body.

planned

Mid-timeframe cross

Price crosses the mid trend body.

planned

Add a market

  1. Bring the market

    Upload any number of timeframe files and name the instrument. The plan needs a 4-hour chart plus daily and weekly at minimum.

  2. It gets kept

    Files are stored in the repository under data/, so a market only ever has to be uploaded once.

  3. Every variation runs

    All 648 combinations, then each one re-checked across 5 separate stretches of history.

  4. The page grows

    A new section appears here with the settings that held, and the full grid underneath.

This page is the record, not the engine — it can't run a test on its own. Saying run <market> in chat is what starts one.
